# The work queue

CONDUCT owns this file and is its only writer. **Exception, 2026-07-31: session BOB
restructured it once, with Bob's explicit authorisation, while CONDUCT was paused for
that purpose.** Ownership returns to CONDUCT with this rewrite; BOB hands
decompositions over rather than editing here (`ORCHESTRATION.md`).

One section per area. An area is **ACTIVE** (holds a worker slot; max two at once) or
**DORMANT** (pre-seeded, promoted when a slot frees). CONDUCT takes the top item whose
status is `queued` and whose depends-on are all `done`, spawns a worker, and on
landing marks it `done`.

**What changed in this rewrite**, so CONDUCT can read it fresh without reconstructing:

- Every item now names its **milestone** (`MILESTONES.md`) and carries an
  **`accepts-when:`** — a command, not a judgment. That was `PLAN.md`'s one
  irreplaceable property and it was lost when the queue replaced it.
- The queue previously held six items while the real forward work was ~40 debt rows,
  six design-doc order-of-work lists and ten CONSTRUCTS steps. Everything is now
  placed in `MILESTONES.md`, and this file carries the runnable slice.
- **RECORD is a new area** (`PARALLELISM.md`): the store core and retrieval, ground
  already being edited with no owner, which is why everything defaulted to CAPTURE.
- **CPDF-2 is SUPERSEDED** by Bob's function-specific Worker topology (I6). Its work
  is not discarded — it becomes the pdf-worker's Tier 2 core.
- **CAP-2 landed** (39a0e1b) and is marked done here; CONDUCT paused before recording
  it.

**BOTH slots are CONTENT-OFFICE**, decided 2026-08-03: Bob confirmed office formats
as the current focus the same day he paused the case-making thread, and his
decomposition names COFF-1 ∥ COFF-2 as "the two behavioural slots" — disjoint files,
genuinely independent. Enacted 2026-08-03 by CONDUCT:

- **Slot 1 · CONTENT-OFFICE, COFF-1** — the FORMAT registry, HTML and PDF moved onto
  it. The D-70 uniformity test, and the step that must precede the format entries or
  they are built twice.
- **Slot 2 · CONTENT-OFFICE, COFF-2** — the OOXML container reader, a pure
  zero-dependency module. Builds against I7 on paper; independent of COFF-1's landing.
- Wave 2 is COFF-3/4/5 (mutually independent; two slots against three items —
  recommended order XLSX → DOCX → PPTX by evidentiary density; final sequencing is
  CONDUCT's). All three additionally wait on IC-1 RESOLVED — done 2026-08-03, see
  `INTERFACE-CHANGES.md`. Slot rotation 2026-08-03 as the wave drained: COFF-3 and
  COFF-4 landed; COFF-5 holds one slot; FRAMEWORK promoted into the other for FW-15
  (the L2→L3 wire — prerequisite for CPDF-10's reading_refs acceptance, and the
  highest-leverage independent item on the board). CPDF-10 additionally waits on
  DEC-35 (the OCR service vendor/account — Bob's; provisional: it stays queued).
- **The case-making run (REC-10 → REC-19 → REC-11 → …, the 2026-08-01 handover order)
  stays QUEUED under a now-DORMANT RECORD**, first in line when a slot frees or Bob
  reopens the thread. Its activation order inside RECORD is unchanged; DEC-8's
  no-act-surface-before-REC-19 doctrine stands.

**Measurement-only and test-estate items hold no slot** (`ORCHESTRATION.md`): COFF-6
(the office-corpus measurement — sets COFF-2's bound, runs IMMEDIATELY), CPDF-9 (the
OCR measurement — gates CPDF-10's whole design, run early) and REC-27/M0-lane work
run out of band whenever CONDUCT has integration capacity between area items.

## BOB INBOX — append-only. BOB writes here; CONDUCT drains it.

The producer/consumer split that makes an architectural change landable WITHOUT
pausing CONDUCT (`ORCHESTRATION.md`). BOB appends; CONDUCT is the sole writer of
everything below this section and drains the inbox as part of its loop, deleting an
entry only once it has been enacted below. The two parties write to disjoint regions,
so neither has to stop for the other.

An entry names: what changed, which queue items it affects, and whether any in-flight
work is superseded. It does NOT decide worker lifecycle — stopping a running worker is
CONDUCT's call.

---

Item format:

    ### <ID> · <queued | active | done | blocked | superseded>
    milestone:        <M0 … M10>
    scope:            <bounded description of the one piece>
    behind-interface: <I1 … I6 | none — what makes it independent>
    depends-on:       <IDs, or none>
    accepts-when:     <a command that passes, plus the negative control that must fail>
    added:            <date · CONDUCT|BOB>
    landed:           <commit, when done>

Standing gate for every item, from `VERIFICATION.md`: `npm run test:battery` green
(every suite, all reported), the item's own `accepts-when:`, the negative control RUN
and recorded in the suite's `NEGATIVE CONTROL:` line, and `npm run test:coverage`
showing no new unreached op.

## M0 — VERIFICATION · cross-cutting, a BACKGROUND LANE (holds no slot)

Test-estate work spanning every area. CONDUCT spawns a worker per item with a claim on
the specific files. These are cheap, they touch no plane behaviour, and they raise the
floor everything else is judged against.

### M0-10 · queued
milestone: M0
scope: **`migrate.test.mjs` fails under concurrency and passes alone — raised by UI-39 rather than smoothed, which is the only reason it is visible.** Observed while the battery ran alongside a coverage run and negative-control runs; green when run by itself. **A suite whose result depends on what else is running is not a suite** — it will eventually fail in someone's integration run and be dismissed as flake, and the first time it is dismissed correctly is indistinguishable from the first time it is dismissed wrongly. **Establish the CAUSE rather than serialising the runner**, which would hide it: shared temp paths, a fixed port, a Miniflare sandbox name, a shared file, or an assumption about wall-clock ordering are the candidates. If the honest fix genuinely is serialisation, say WHY at the site and make the reason survive — a suite pinned to serial for an unrecorded reason is debt nobody can pay off. **SWEEP FOR THE CLASS:** this is a test-estate item, so the question is whether any OTHER suite shares the resource this one contends on. The battery's own temp accounting already reports sandbox and orphan counts — use it as an instrument rather than adding a new one.
behind-interface: none — test estate, no plane behaviour
depends-on: none
accepts-when: the cause NAMED and recorded at the site, not merely worked around; `cd bio-plane && npm run test:battery` green **run concurrently with `node scripts/coverage.mjs --strict`**, repeated enough times to be evidence rather than one lucky pass, and the number of repetitions STATED; `$?` read unpiped on both. NEGATIVE CONTROL run and recorded — reintroduce the contention and the failure returns, so the fix is shown to be the fix rather than a timing change that moved it.
added: 2026-08-05 · CONDUCT (UI-39's delegation; test estate, holds no slot)

### M0-11 · queued
milestone: M0
scope: **`op=publishedcase`'s loose branch (`#looseEditionState`) is driven by NO suite in the plane's battery** — ratified bytes that belong to no case. Found by UI-35 while measuring the op's published shape, and reported rather than quietly relied upon: its answer shape is currently asserted only from `civicos-ui`, which means **the plane's own battery cannot tell you if that branch changes**, and a surface suite is the wrong place for the plane's contract to live. This is the "test through the op" rule with the op half missing — the same shape as `op=invitelook` shipping with a ReferenceError while 1276 assertions passed. Drive the branch in the plane's battery and assert what it answers, including that its top-level keys are the SAME single success return the case path uses (UI-35 measured that they are, from source; assert it end to end). **SWEEP FOR THE CLASS:** the question is not this one branch — it is **which other op branches the battery never drives**, and `scripts/coverage.mjs` counts ops rather than branches, so it cannot see this. Say plainly what your instrument can and cannot see.
behind-interface: I3 (asserted against, not changed)
depends-on: none
accepts-when: `cd bio-plane && npm run test:battery` green with the loose branch DRIVEN end to end and its answer asserted — measure your own baseline first; any delta attributed per suite; `node scripts/coverage.mjs --strict` run DIRECTLY, `$?` unpiped, exit 0. NEGATIVE CONTROL run and recorded — break the loose branch's answer and the plane's OWN battery fails naming it (today only the UI harness would), which is the whole point of the item.
added: 2026-08-05 · CONDUCT (UI-35's delegation; test estate, holds no slot)

### M0-12 · queued
milestone: M0
scope: **A comment naming `op=<name>` must be checked against the dispatch table — the mechanical defence against the class that has now cost a full queue item.** REC-58 was queued on a sentence in IC-22's SETTLED text saying `op=publishcase` returns `opened`. It does not and never did, and the whole item went to establishing that. **This is REC-41's lesson for the third time (right about the FIELD, wrong about the OP), and it is now mechanically checkable**, which is the only reason it is worth an item rather than a resolution to be careful: `DO_PATH` and the store's dispatch keys are both readable, so a check can assert that **every `op=<name>` appearing in a comment or a planning document names an op that actually exists, and that the method a comment attributes behaviour to is the one the dispatch table routes that op to.** The second half is the load-bearing one — **`op=publish` is an ALIAS mapped to `publishcase`, so the op whose NAME matches the spreading method is routed away from it**, and a check that only verified existence would have passed the false sentence. Cover both `bio-plane/**` comments and `docs/development/**`, since the sentence that caused this was in a planning document rather than in code. **STATE WHAT THE INSTRUMENT CANNOT SEE**, because REC-58 measured the limit precisely: a field arriving through a SPREAD declares no key, so a source-level reader cannot see it — say so at the site rather than letting the check read as complete.
behind-interface: none — test estate and hygiene, no plane behaviour
depends-on: none
accepts-when: `cd bio-plane && npm run test:battery` green — **measure your own baseline first; four briefs this week carried a stale figure and every worker was right to trust its own measurement** — any delta attributed per suite; with an assertion that no comment or planning document names a nonexistent op, and that an op named as routing to a method routes to THAT method; `node scripts/coverage.mjs --strict` run DIRECTLY, `$?` unpiped, exit 0. NEGATIVE CONTROLS run and recorded — (1) restore IC-22's false sentence and the check FAILS naming the file, the op and the method it actually routes to; (2) plant a comment naming an op that does not exist and it FAILS naming it; (3) **neuter the walk and assert its REACH as a DELTA with the corpus size PRINTED** — the headline assertion of three separate walks this week still passed over an empty corpus, twice inside the instrument built to prevent it; (4) an over-strictness arm — a correct comment phrased unlike anything you wrote must PASS.
added: 2026-08-05 · CONDUCT (routed out of REC-58; test estate, holds no slot)

### M0-8 · done
milestone: M0
scope: **Stop the battery leaking temp directories (D-186).** Three parts: (a) the
  miniflare harness disposes of its sandbox in a `finally`, so a THROWING suite still
  cleans up — the leak is worst exactly when tests fail; (b) each suite that mints its
  own sandbox disposes of it the same way; (c) `scripts/battery.mjs` sweeps ORPHANS at
  start — older than the current run only, never a blanket wipe, because a concurrent
  worker's live sandbox must survive (that is not hypothetical: it was live during the
  cleanup that found this). The OCR probes' npm-installs into temp are bounded
  separately and may stay, since they are probes and not battery.
behind-interface: none — test estate
depends-on: none
accepts-when: `cd bio-plane && npm run test:battery` green twice in a row with the
  `$TMPDIR` `miniflare-*` count NOT growing between runs (that comparison IS the
  assertion); a suite made to throw mid-run still leaves no sandbox behind; negative
  control — remove the `finally` disposal and the count grows again by the number of
  miniflare instances, which is the state today.
added: 2026-08-04 · BOB
landed: (merge on main, worker 7b39d33) — **THE ITEM'S PREMISE WAS INVERTED AND THE WORKER MEASURED IT**: the leak is worst on SUCCESS, not failure. Measured 3 modes x 3 runs — succeed-and-exit leaked 3/3, throw-mid-run leaked 0/3. Miniflare#dispose() disarms its own exit hook and then calls removeDir FIRE-AND-FORGET without awaiting; every suite ends `await dispose(); process.exit()`, which hygiene REQUIRES so a lingering handle cannot turn green into a hang — so process.exit beats the unawaited removal with the safety net already down, while a THROWING suite never reaches dispose() and miniflare's still-armed hook cleans up perfectly. **A `finally` would have fixed nothing.** 23,263 directories was the record of a battery working correctly, scaling with GREEN integration throughput. THE FIX: test/sandbox.mjs owns $TMPDIR for the process and rmSyncs it in process.on('exit') + SIGINT/TERM/HUP — SYNCHRONOUS is the point, exit listeners run to completion so they cannot be outrun; part (b) falls out structurally (every mkdtemp lands inside the owned dir); 84 suites take one import line; hygiene DERIVES the requirement from source so the list cannot fall behind the directory (D-93's failure mode), which matters because ~80 test:<suite> scripts run outside the battery. THE SWEEP, three rules because the two kinds carry different evidence: bio-battery-<pid>-* names its owner so kill(pid,0) decides with NO CLOCK (EPERM reads as ALIVE — the one error that must); legacy prefixes by age at runStart−2h GRACE (plain older-than-this-run is unsafe: a battery that began 60s earlier has older directories and is still using them); and over both, anything a live workerd/node still holds a descriptor in is spared outright — NOT THEORETICAL: a suite from another live worktree was found running at 1h28m and survived all seven sweeps, verified by name. THE ASSERTION MADE ATTRIBUTABLE: the first version went RED ON A GREEN RUN (a foreign un-fixed battery writing into the same $TMPDIR) — a false red on the battery is worse than the leak it reports — so the runner mkdtemps its own sandbox and spawns every suite with TMPDIR pointing at it; residue there is THIS run's leak exactly, while the host count is still reported and attributed. Battery green TWICE (95/95, 5172; 0 directories both runs); --strict 129/129. NCs RUN x2 + a driven throw (comment out the exit hook → the runner exits 1 naming 84 leaked sandboxes WHILE ALL 95 SUITES REPORT GREEN AND ALL 5172 ASSERTIONS PASS — precisely why this ran unnoticed for weeks, and why the instrument belongs in the runner not a suite; strip an import → hygiene names the file). D-186 CLOSED.

### M0-7 · done
milestone: M0
scope: The END-TO-END pipeline integration test — the whole M4 entity-axis chain driven THROUGH THE CONTROL PLANE in ONE suite, proving the pieces COMPOSE. Each op is unit-tested in isolation; nothing proves a field one op writes is the field the next op reads — and a DEPLOY exercises that composition for the first time. Chain one document's journey: `op=acquire` (carries profile+digests+reading, FW-3/4/5) → `op=promote` (reading persists) → `op=entitycreate`+`op=resolve` (reference resolves to the entity at its §8.1 grade, FW-6/7) → the REC-5 alarm tick (connections auto-derive) → `op=connections` shows the graded connection → `op=progressiondefine`+`op=thread` (FW-8/9) → `op=instance` shows the instance, its weakest-grade, and a missing-predecessor finding → `op=proposals` surfaces it (REC-6) → `op=proposedispose` ages it (REC-7). ASSERT the JOIN KEYS line up across stages (capture_sha, entity_id, progression_key/stage_key) — that each stage consumes the prior stage's actual output, not a fixture. This is "test through the op, verify live" applied end-to-end short of a real deploy, and it is the evidence a deploy decision rests on. Adds NO op.

milestone: M2
scope: **D-200 — ten live bundles claim a route they cannot show.** `op=audit` on the live instance answers 31 checked / 21 clean / **10 with errors, every one `C-18.9`**: *"provenance documents[0] is at or past verified with no provenance_chain: a published hash claims these bytes came from somewhere by some route, and this document names none"*. MEASURED 2026-08-04 against the live plane and established as PRE-EXISTING RECORD STATE rather than deploy damage — `C-18.9` appears identically in the 0.55.0 and the 0.56.0 bundle, so the previous build would have reported the same ten. **This is the overclaim class this project ranks worst, sitting in live DATA where no code fix reaches it.** DIST's gate step 8 wants `op=audit` clean and it is not, so this item is what makes that honest. **DO NOT CLOSE IT BY WEAKENING `C-18.9` — the check is right and the record is wrong.** Decide PER BUNDLE, on evidence: either the chain is reconstructible from what was ACTUALLY captured (the capture rows, the R2 bytes, the CDX record) and is written from that evidence with each hop naming what produced it, or the honest act is to move the document BACK OFF `verified` and say so. **Back-dating a chain nobody recorded is exactly the invention the gate exists to prevent, and is forbidden here by name**; a bundle whose chain cannot be reconstructed is `undetermined`, and undetermined is first-class and must be STATED rather than quietly left at `verified`. Apply dispositions through the plane's OWN ops, never by hand-written SQL — if no op can move a document off `verified`, that absence is itself a finding: report it and route it rather than reaching around the plane. **SWEEP FOR THE CLASS, do not fix the ten:** eight items this week each found more than their brief predicted, so the questions this item must answer are (a) whether the ten share a WRITE PATH that lets a document reach `verified` with no chain — if one does, that path is the defect and the ten are its symptom; (b) whether any OTHER check tolerates a record state it cannot support; and (c) whether `op=audit`'s report can distinguish *no chain recorded* from *a chain recorded and empty*, which are different facts about the record and must not read alike.

### REC-55 · done
milestone: M8
scope: **The refuse-weight gate cannot see a query selection that moved — routed out of UI-25 as a DELEGATION, and CONDUCT decided the mechanism rather than raising it.** MEASURED by UI-25 against `store.mjs`: `selectionResolve` computes `stopped = moved && weight === "refuse"`, and `moved = revised.length + removed + added > 0` — **`digestChanged` is not a term in it.** So a `kind:"query"` selection whose membership swaps AT A CONSTANT COUNT reports `moved:false`, and a refuse-weight act passes the gate silently over a set the operator did not see. `retire` and `release` are both refuse-weight and both take a selection handle. That is precisely "a state transition landing on a set the operator did not see", which `selectionResolve`'s OWN comment says the refuse weight exists to prevent. **Nothing is exposed today** — the finder offers only `cite`, which is report-weight — so this is LATENT, and latent is why it is worth fixing now rather than after a surface offers a refuse-weight act over a query lease. **THE FIX IS DECIDED, and the reasoning is recorded here rather than sent to Bob because it is a mechanism call, which is CONDUCT's:** do NOT fold `digestChanged` into `moved` — `moved` correctly means per-row movement and every enumerated caller depends on that meaning. Have the REFUSE GATE test `moved || digestChanged`. UI-25's recommendation, adopted; it changes one predicate at one site and leaves the published `moved` semantics that two surfaces read. **CORRECTED 2026-08-05 at integration: this scope originally said SIX and that figure was not reproducible against the tree.** REC-55 measured `app.html` reading a selection's `moved` at exactly **two** sites (`selectionMoved`, `FINDSEL.moved`); the other two `.moved` hits are an unrelated field on a different op. CONDUCT wrote the number without measuring it. The ruling is unaffected — not folding is still right — but a figure stated as though measured, and not measured, is the same failure as the ~4,900 line count that stood for weeks. **SWEEP FOR THE CLASS:** the question is not this one predicate — it is every place the plane decides whether a selection is safe to act on, and whether any OTHER gate reads `moved` where it means "has this answer changed at all". Expect more than one; six items this week each found more than their brief predicted.

### REC-57 · done
milestone: M8
scope: **Two plane ops publish a bound they do not name, forcing every consumer to guess — the UI-39 class, one layer down (UI → RECORD delegation).** MEASURED by UI-39 against the plane: (a) **`op=readingname` caps at 100/500 and publishes neither a `limit` nor a truncation flag**, and its `count` is the length of what it SENT — indistinguishable from what EXISTS, which is precisely the ambiguity that makes an unstated bound read as completeness. UI-39 consumed what was there and could go no further honestly; this is the half that must move in the plane. **The consumer is a COMPLETENESS CLAIM in both cases**, which is why this is not cosmetic: UI-25's whole item existed because a member with more than 500 hits could cite only the first 500 into a case. **SWEEP FOR THE CLASS:** the question is not these two ops — it is **every capped op, and whether it publishes what its cap was and whether it truncated.** Read the roster of capped ops off `store.mjs`/`query.mjs` the way UI-39's walk does rather than listing them by hand, and where an op already publishes the fact, say so rather than adding a second spelling of it. milestone: M10
scope: **`op=publishcase` still publishes `case.opened`** — **FALSE, and corrected 2026-08-05 at integration: it does not, and never did.** CONDUCT copied this premise out of IC-22's own SETTLED text without re-measuring it, and IC-22 is now corrected in place. `op=publishcase` dispatches to `Store.publishCase()`, which computes no `opened`, reads none and returns none. **This is REC-41's lesson for the THIRD time — right about the FIELD, wrong about the OP** — and it is left standing here with its correction rather than rewritten, because the item was spent establishing it and the next reader should see how a false sentence in a settled interface record became a queue item. ### REC-4 · done
milestone: M8
scope: The server-side TASK-ACTOR FENCE (lifted from UI-1's delegation). Today `taskResolve`/`taskForward` (`store.mjs` ~5299) refuse no-actor / no-such-task / already-resolved, but do NOT refuse a member who is neither the task's `assignee` nor an admin — any member-class credential can resolve or forward ANY task by id (the actor is stamped honestly into history, so it is traceable, but not PREVENTED). The TASK construct makes the refusal an accountability rule ("this is not yours to resolve, and here is who it is with"), and UI-1 renders that refusal — but it is COSMETIC until the plane enforces it. Add the fence in the plane, for BOTH `taskResolve` and `taskForward`: a caller who is neither the assignee nor an admin is refused with a NAMED reason mirroring the construct's refusal shape. RECONCILE with D-98's routing doctrine before over-fencing: an `unassigned` task is meant to be claimable by the routed role — check what the routing intends (member_expertise → PM → group admin) and keep that path open; the admin override stays. This is an I3 addition (a new named refusal reason), not a reshape.
behind-interface: I3
depends-on: none
accepts-when: a member who is neither the assignee nor an admin is refused `taskResolve`/`taskForward` on another's assigned task with the named reason; the assignee and an admin still succeed; an unassigned task stays claimable per D-98 routing; negative control — remove the fence and the non-assignee resolve succeeds again.

BIG FIND: the ops were machine-credential-only (not in SESSION_OPS), so UI-1 was DEAD against the real plane (its mock never hit the op auth); REC-4 opened both to member/admin SESSIONS (TASK_ACTIONS→SESSION_OPS, NEEDS=null, identity-not-capability) — a spoofed body actor cannot pass (server stamps from session), and a machine credential is now fenced off assigned tasks. And **FOUR of the seven were caught only by RE-RUNNING the sweep after the main commit**, not by the read that produced it — the seventh independent measurement this session that a careful read undercounts. Bob's own quotation left verbatim with the vocabulary reconciled after it; struck sentences STRUCK rather than deleted. Battery 98/98 (5567), the +26 attributed to publish (+14) and multifinding (+12) with the nine fixture-edited suites gaining ZERO, which is what makes the attribution checkable; hygiene 650; coverage strict run directly, exit 0, 272 arms. **ONE PROCESS NOTE THE WORKER REPORTED AGAINST ITSELF:** its first battery ran piped into `tail` and reported exit 0 while five suites had failed — CLAUDE.md's own warning, added to that file earlier the same day, hit by the next worker to run. Grade VALUES wait on this measurement; the doctrine (fidelity bounds the CAPTURE axis; no machine mints the grade; member attestation is the only route to the top) is already stated and the measurement must not be read as permission. ### CPDF-10 · queued
milestone: M2
scope: **The Tier-3 OCR path, behind whatever placement CPDF-9's measurement permits.** PLACEMENT RE-BASED BY DEC-35 (2026-08-04, superseding the 2026-08-03 service-first note): in-plane and pdf-worker stay RULED OUT by bundle size; the IN-ACCOUNT path is the DEFAULT and its engine is now TESSERACT AS A FLEET MEMBER pending CPDF-12's deployed probe (DEC-42: Moondream returned NO-GO on coordinates; tesseract was never blocked on size, only on the Free CPU ceiling Paid removes) — sovereign instances must not need a second vendor account (the D-115 class); the EXTERNAL service (Azure DI Read primary) is the ESCALATION tier or the fallback on NO-GO; NOTHING FUNDED. Per-region confidence becomes confidence-WHERE-SUPPLIED, else a stated confidence: none in the chain with the fidelity cap set by measurement; pseudo-confidence (a self-reported number thresholded as calibrated) FORBIDDEN as the costs-nothing class; measured self-refusal (CPDF-11's degradation ladder) is the only earnable per-region trigger. The provenance chain and the image-region anchor UNCHANGED, non-negotiable. Deps gain CPDF-11 and (on GO) CPDF-12. D-152, DEC-4 as twice amended. THE PROVENANCE RULE IS THE ITEM, not the engine: **`text_source` records a CHAIN, not a token** — `pixels → ocr(engine, version) → ai(function, version) → attested(member, date, extent)` — each step naming what performed it, and **each step can only weaken the claim, never strengthen it** (an AI that cleans a garbled line produced more READABLE text, not more RELIABLE text; the hazard of this capability is output that looks better than its input — do not let the chain collapse to a single label). A text LAYER is itself an unverified transcription (`pdfstructure.mjs` already decodes through the file's own `/ToUnicode` map), so **the ceiling is VERIFIED AGAINST THE RENDERED IMAGE, reachable from both paths**: member attestation is offered on a text layer too, SCOPED to what was actually checked (a leg citing outside the attested extent does not inherit it); the chain is still recorded — verification supersedes it as grade determinant, never as record. Attestation is a member act refusable to a machine credential. Transcription fidelity BOUNDS the capture axis (weakest link of byte provenance and fidelity) — no third scale, no new machinery. A basis leg resting on OCR'd text carries its image region (page + rect); OCR never raises a capture grade; a low-confidence region reads `undetermined`, never a best guess. Text reaches the READING path via FW-15's wire.
behind-interface: I2
depends-on: CPDF-9, COFF-1, CPDF-11, CPDF-12-on-GO (dependency corrected 2026-08-03: the handover's "CPDF-8" was RECONCILED §3.3's name for the FORMAT registry, carried as COFF-1. The page-to-pixels rendering path BOB flagged as the other candidate reading is real but is DECIDED by CPDF-9's placement measurement — an external-service placement needs no renderer; an in-plane or fleet placement does — so the renderer item is named when that recommendation lands, not pre-built.)
accepts-when: `cd bio-plane && npm run test:battery` green with a real image-only Oakland PDF yielding text whose `text_source` chain names each step with per-region confidence and reaching `reading_refs`, while a text-layer PDF yields its own honest chain and the two are distinguishable in the projection, the index and an export; an attestation is refused to a machine credential and scoped to its extent; negative control — strip the `text_source` marker and the suite fails naming an OCR'd document indistinguishable from a published text layer; drop the confidence floor so a garbled region emits a best guess and the suite fails; collapse the chain to one label and the suite fails.

### CPDF-11 · done
milestone: M2
scope: **Measure Moondream 3.1 (Workers AI, `env.AI`) as the IN-ACCOUNT OCR path —
  measurement-only, commits no product code, holds no slot, costs nothing to run
  (no account, no credential, no signup).** Against the SAME ground-truthed Oakland
  exhibit and instrument CPDF-9 used, so the numbers are comparable line for line.
  Four things, and the last two carry more weight here than for any classic engine
  because Moondream is GENERATIVE and its failure mode is text that looks better
  than its input:
  (a) **Accuracy** against the measured local-tesseract floor — 99.96% character
  accuracy, 90/90 digits, ZERO minted digits (MEASUREMENTS.md 2026-08-03). Digits
  are where OCR fails and where human checking fails too (DEC-4).
  (b) **Coordinates VERIFIED, not assumed** — the model claims bounding boxes;
  confirm they ALIGN with the text they are returned for, because the image-region
  anchor is what lets a reader check a claim against pixels and an attester testify
  to it. This is the one non-negotiable structural requirement.
  (c) **The blank-page negative control, weighted heavily** — a blank and a
  noise-only page must yield NOTHING, never plausible text. An engine that
  hallucinates on noise is the one failure mode that would put invented text in the
  record.
  (d) **The degradation ladder — this is the new measurement and it decides the
  confidence question.** Feed progressively degraded regions of the ground-truthed
  page and score whether the model REFUSES (says illegible / returns nothing) or
  INVENTS. Only measured refusal-reliability licenses structured self-refusal as a
  per-region trigger. **Do NOT ask the model for a confidence score and threshold
  it** — a self-reported number treated as calibrated is the costs-nothing-to-
  produce class and is FORBIDDEN by DEC-35.
  Record in `MEASUREMENTS.md` with date and instrument; state the recommended
  transcription-fidelity CAP for this engine (DEC-4: no machine mints the grade, and
  the measurement sets the ceiling).
behind-interface: none — it commits no code
depends-on: none
accepts-when: `MEASUREMENTS.md` carries character accuracy, digit accuracy, minted-
  digit count, coordinate-alignment verdict, blank/noise-page result and the
  degradation-ladder refuse-vs-invent table — each with date and instrument — plus a
  stated recommended cap and a GO/NO-GO on the in-account path; negative control —
  the blank page and the noise page, run and reported, with any non-empty output
  reported as a FAILURE of the path rather than a curiosity.
added: 2026-08-04 · BOB
landed: (merge on main, worker 9d6274a) — model @cf/moondream/moondream3.1-9B-A2B, reached through a scratch Worker carrying env.AI (the project token has no Workers AI REST scope — measured, 403 vs 200), uploaded/used/DELETED-and-verified-gone; account pinned before any upload; nothing funded, ~$0.019 of tokens. VS THE FLOOR: 99.44% char (floor 99.96%), 89/90 digits (the miss an OMISSION of a page number, not a misread), 0 minted at full resolution — and NOT REPRODUCIBLE (3 runs of identical bytes, 2 distinct transcriptions). COORDINATES: FAIL, verified not assumed — 2 of 24 box-checks align, task=query returns no boxes with text, and it returned a CONFIDENT box for $99,999,999.99 which is not on the page. CONTROLS PASS (blank 3/3 + noise 3/3 → ILLEGIBLE, zero non-empty). THE LADDER'S SHAPE: refusal is a FLOOR ALARM ONE RUNG PAST THE INVENTION BAND, not a boundary alarm — R3 (75dpi+blur) refused 0/3 with 16–20 MINTED digits per run in fluent prose indistinguishable from a clean run ($50,000 → $10,000; 'lowest responsible bidder' → 'least responsible'), R4 refused 3/3. So measured refusal-reliability does NOT license structured self-refusal; the cap carries everything. CAP: C, NEVER B (non-reproducible; no checkable region; R3 indistinguishable from R0) + a rule the cap does not reach: a digit from this engine may not carry a leg without member attestation. VERDICT: NO-GO as the default path on the non-negotiable. THE LIVE ALTERNATIVE, CONDUCT's sequencing ruling at integration: the COMPOSED shape (detect → crop → transcribe the crop) scored 99.62%, 10/10 digits, 0 minted on the one trustworthy region — n=1, UNPROVEN NOT REFUTED, and the ONLY in-account shape that can carry the anchor; it needs the renderer, so closing CPDF-12 and measuring it are ALTERNATIVES — CPDF-12 is RE-SCOPED to the composed-shape measurement (still in DEC-35's in-account-first preference, still nothing funded) and CPDF-10 waits on ITS verdict before the external tier is funded. Two instrument findings kept (both wrong instruments gave the MORE FLATTERING number — the displaced-box criterion measured text density; the referee window was 47% placeable); comparability with CPDF-9 ENFORCED not claimed (the probe imports the floor's ground truth and exits if a metric expression moved). milestone: M2

THE OBSERVATION THAT MAY REMOVE THE RENDERER ENTIRELY, to be VERIFIED not assumed: on the measured exhibit each page is ONE full-page embedded image (JPEG DCTDecode, CCITT G4) — for the image-only class, which is what OCR is FOR, that means image EXTRACTION not page rasterisation; check it across the corpus BEFORE building a renderer, and if it holds the expensive half is a JPEG pass-through plus a CCITT G4 decoder. **AND DEC-41 SEQUENCES WITH IT (2026-08-04): the published container's PDF renderings are this item's other consumer** — the manifest's reserved layout.note join becomes REAL (a server-side renderer produces the PDFs; they join parts[] with their hashes in published_shas), because both needs want page-shaped output from workerd and building two renderers would be the D-164 lesson repeated. Every page carries DEC-34's full header PLUS DEC-40's filter line. Prior scope, kept as the reasoning that got here: the verdict's composed shape (detect the block → crop → transcribe the crop) is the only in-account shape that can carry the image-region anchor, scored 99.62%/10-of-10/0-minted at n=1, and NEEDS this renderer — so the item is now (a) the renderer measurement-first (bundle/CPU per the CPDF-9 pattern; a fleet member the likely placement, I6) and (b) THE COMPOSED-SHAPE PROBE at n>>1 against the same enforced-comparable floor (reproducibility across runs, minted digits, anchor checkability per crop — the three NO-GO reasons re-measured under the composed shape). Still measurement + instrument; product code only for the renderer if its own numbers permit.   no group discovers it as a surprise). (d) The ASYMMETRIC drift handler above: a
  worse calibration raises a re-evaluation obligation naming exactly the affected
  transcriptions and re-grades NOTHING; a better one raises nothing. (e) An
  announcement watch is OPTIONAL and may only shorten the interval to the next
  probe — it may never stand IN for one, and it may never itself change a grade.

### CAP-4 · done
milestone: M2
scope: `CAPTURE-SCALING.md` item 6, DECIDED 2026-07-31 under Bob's delegation — read that item before building, it carries four specifics. (a) Post-hoc reuse verification from `site_assets`, unconditional, costs zero requests, verdicts appended and dated. (b) At ratification, re-fetch every reused part: MANDATORY as an attempt and a record, never as agreement — `confirmed` / `changed` / `unavailable` all ratify, and ratifying with a reused part while saying nothing is what is forbidden. (c) A PLAIN GET, not `If-None-Match`: both cost one subrequest, and a 304 is the origin's assertion where a hash is our own evidence. This deliberately reverses the conditional-GET suggestion elsewhere in that document, which is right for working capture and wrong here. (d) A fourth outcome `not_attempted`, recorded with its reason, for parts the invocation's budget could not reach — bounded by the calibrated ceiling in `capture_limits`, never silently omitted.

### FW-7 · done
milestone: M4
scope: CONSTRUCTS Step 4, SLICE B — the RECOGNISERS. Resolve a reading reference (`reading_refs`, FW-5, a raw `kind:key`) to a registry entity (`entities`/`entity_aliases`, FW-6), and DECLARE THE METHOD, which IS the §8.1 connection grade (framework §8.1): **A** = the source's own identifier, both ends captured; **B** = an identifier the source uses, matched exactly in captured content at both ends; **C** = correspondence (a name/title/date proximity), NEVER presented as established and flagged for a member to confirm. Store each resolution (`capture_sha`/reading_ref → `entity_id`, with grade + method) in a new table (I5, additive). This delivers the reverse index — "every document that concerns this entity" — the single largest piece of manual work the framework removes. Undetermined is first-class: a reference matching NO entity stays honestly UNRESOLVED (never force-matched); a Grade C correspondence is flagged for confirmation, never shown as established (an equality that costs nothing is not evidence). A declared relation (FW-6) is constitutive and stays OUTSIDE this grade — do NOT grade relations. Grade is IMPROVABLE (§8.1: a C becomes B when a shared identifier is found) — model the resolution so its grade can be RAISED, not frozen.
behind-interface: I5
depends-on: FW-5, FW-6
accepts-when: a reading reference matching an entity's alias resolves to it at grade C, and one matching a source identifier at both ends resolves at grade A/B; "every document concerning entity X" returns the captures whose readings resolve to X; a reference matching nothing is honestly unresolved; negative control — a Grade C resolution is not reported as established, AND dropping the resolver empties the reverse index for a document known to concern X.

Grade IMPROVABLE in place (C→A via raised_from; #upsertResolution never downgrades, never duplicates). Grade C STRUCTURALLY can't read established. op=concerns = the reverse index "every document that concerns entity X". op=resolvetestify holds member grade-D testimony (author+date, never machine-minted). 4 ops (resolve/resolvetestify mutating contribute + resolutions/concerns reads). I5 1.3.0 additive. purge covers it (both arms, D-113). battery 58/58, coverage --strict 97/97. NCs RUN (force C established → 3 fail; neuter upsert → reverse index empties). ### UI-38 · queued
milestone: M8
scope: **The SURFACE REGISTRY and the RECIPE format with their build-time validation — `ASSISTANT-PILOT.md` §7 step 1, and it needs no AI at all.** Bob picked DEC-27's assistant as the first AI integration (2026-08-04) and the design's load-bearing choice is that the assistant's "training" is **SELF-DESCRIPTION, not a document** — five layers ordered by drift rate. This item builds the two that drift fastest, and it pays for itself as documentation the day it lands whether or not an assistant is ever built. (a) **Surfaces SELF-DESCRIBE**: every member-facing surface in `civicos-ui/` carries an id and a machine-readable statement of what it is for and which acts it offers, and **the HARNESS enforces that the described set EQUALS the real set** — a surface that exists and describes nothing, and a description naming a surface that does not exist, must each FAIL the build. (b) **Recipes are DATA, never prose**: a recipe is an ordered list of steps whose steps NAME surface ids and plane ops, and **the build FAILS when a step names a surface or an op that does not exist** — the D-106 defence applied to assistant knowledge, and the entire reason this is worth building before any AI exists. Take the op names from **the registry the plane already EMITS** (that layer cannot drift), never from a hand-typed list: **a hand copy agrees at zero cost and this project has measured that five times on five subjects**, so the drift assertion must be STRUCTURAL. Two constraints from the design that a build session would otherwise lose: the ~305 teaching-grade refusal `detail:` strings are the answer key and are surfaced VERBATIM, never paraphrased; and an answer reporting absence must name its LEVEL (meaning / content / documents / internet), so a surface's self-description must make its level legible rather than leaving it to the caller. **SWEEP FOR THE CLASS:** the question is not whether the surfaces you start with are described, it is whether a NEW one can be added silently.
behind-interface: I3
depends-on: none
accepts-when: `node civicos-ui/test/run.mjs` green with every surface described and the described set proved equal to the real set **in both directions**; at least one authored recipe whose every step resolves to a real surface id and a real EMITTED op, with the op names read from the plane's own registry and that sourcing asserted (a hand-typed list must fail the assertion); `cd bio-plane && npm run test:battery` stays green — 100/100 and 5,664 today, any delta attributed per suite; `node scripts/coverage.mjs --strict` run DIRECTLY with `$?` read unpiped, exit 0. NEGATIVE CONTROLS run and recorded: (1) a recipe step naming an op the plane does not emit FAILS the build, naming the step AND the op; (2) a recipe step naming a surface id that does not exist FAILS naming it; (3) add a real surface, describe it nowhere, and the harness FAILS; (4) **neuter the walk that discovers surfaces and the REACH assertion fails as a DELTA** — a walk that covers nothing passes everything, and that class has eight sightings this week including one instrument that compared a planted count to 1; (5) the pins must go RED when a described-but-absent surface is introduced and GREEN when it is removed, not the reverse.
added: 2026-08-05 · CONDUCT (enacting the ASSISTANT-PILOT inbox entry the 2026-08-04 handover left undrained; §7 steps 2–6 stay unqueued behind this one, since the registry is what they validate against and D-199 already carries step 3's `ai`-class design)

### UI-39 · done
milestone: M8
scope: **The four bound-dropping sites UI-25 found and REPORTED rather than edited (they sat outside its claim).** Same class as UI-25's five: *the plane published a bound and the surface dropped it*, which lets a member read a page as the whole. Ordered by consequence, and **the second one is the sharp one:** (a) `heldMatch` searches at the plane's DEFAULT of 50 and **DECIDES WHETHER TO WRITE A DUPLICATE BUNDLE on that read** — a bound silently governing a WRITE, not merely a display, so a match past the fiftieth row produces a duplicate the record then carries; treat this as the item's centre. (b) `loadResolveCandidates` is capped at 100 by the plane and its long bound sentence states only SEMANTIC bounds and never the numeric one — UI-26 measured that an unstated bound reads as completeness and uses no completeness word, so the word-screen stays green over it. (c) `op=queue` / `op=tasks` are capped at 200 with `counts` published and read nowhere. scope: **`scripts/coverage.mjs` can report that a suite declares NO negative control when it declares an elaborate one — and can record only the first arm of one that has five (found by REC-48).** The control detector is `/NEGATIVE CONTROL:\s*(.+?)\s*(?:\*\/|$)/` run against `src.split("\n").slice(0, 60).join("\n")`. **CORRECTED 2026-08-04 AT THIS ITEM'S OWN LANDING — MY RE-MEASUREMENT WAS WRONG AND REC-48'S ORIGINAL REPORT WAS RIGHT.** I wrote here that `\s*` crosses newlines so a wrapped control still matches, and that the 60-line window was what bit REC-48. The first half is false for the shape that actually occurs: my synthetic put the comment's `*/` on the SAME line as the text, and a real multi-arm block closes on its OWN line — at which point `(.+?)` cannot reach `*/` and `$` is nowhere, so it matches NOTHING. Re-verified both shapes side by side at integration. **The 60-line window IS a genuine second defect** (`readingname.test.mjs` is its victim: a 4-line declaration beginning on line 59, entirely outside the window, so the register quoted its one-liner instead) — it simply is not what bit REC-48. These suites carry long doctrine headers, so when REC-48 made its control block longer the marker crossed line 60 and the strict run went from exit 0 to exit 1 at 96 of 98, naming two suites whose controls had just got BETTER. Measured directly: marker on line 59 with content on line 61 → no match; marker past line 60 → no match. And a second, quieter defect in the same line: `(.+?)` captures only the FIRST line, so a five-arm control is recorded as one arm — the instrument reads as fully green while stating a fraction of what it checked. That is the generous direction, which is the one failure this instrument exists to prevent. FIX: find the declaration wherever it is in the file, capture the whole block, and assert the instrument's own reach — the same reach discipline this project has now needed four times (UI-30, REC-49, UI-28, REC-48's own first draft). Do NOT paper over it with a convention that the marker line must be self-contained; REC-48 worked around it that way to land, and a convention propping up an instrument is the thing to remove.
behind-interface: none
depends-on: none
accepts-when: `node scripts/coverage.mjs --strict` run DIRECTLY with `$?` read unpiped, exit 0, and the suite count of declared controls UNCHANGED at 98/98 — with REC-48's multi-line block restored to the shape it wanted, proving the fix rather than the workaround; a self-test asserting the detector finds a control declared past line 60 and captures every arm of a multi-arm block; negative control — hide a suite's control declaration entirely and the strict run fails NAMING that suite, and separately truncate a multi-arm block and the count of arms it reports drops.

So `\s*` reaches a close that sits ALONE on the next line and matches nothing when the next line carries TEXT — which is what a multi-arm block is. All three defects are closed anyway, because the window is real and separate. **WHAT WAS SILENTLY UNDER-READ ALL ALONG, and this is the finding no review would have produced:** 14 of 98 suites had a fuller declaration the detector could not see; THREE were registered with fewer arms than they state (`reading-wire` 2 of 3, `reuse-ratify` 4 of 5, `setup-honesty` 3 of 5); and `readingname.test.mjs` is the WINDOW's own victim — its 4-line, 2,233-character declaration begins on line 59 and fell entirely outside, so the register quoted an 870-character one-liner instead. Across the tree the old detector read 234 arms and the new one reads 251. THREE JUDGEMENTS MADE AND RECORDED IN `scripts/control-register.mjs`'s header. **Block extent**: marker → its comment's close OR the first blank comment line, whichever comes first, because a declaration is a PARAGRAPH — several suites state the control as one paragraph of a longer header, and running to the close would quote back prose the suite never declared; it also stops at a second marker so adjacent declarations cannot merge. **Which declaration**: M0-2's backfill left most suites stating the control TWICE (header prose plus the one-line register entry, the same control), so every block is found and the recorded one is the FULLEST SINGLE BLOCK, NEVER THE SUM — summing two statements of one control credits it twice, which is the generous direction wearing a different hat. **Arms are REPORTED, NEVER GATED**, one whitespace-delimited `->` being the register grammar's own separator, deliberately syntactic and stated as such: `--strict` still fails on exactly what it always failed on, a suite with no declaration, because a floor on arm counts is strictness nobody has ruled on and over-strict is as bad as generous. REC-48's blocks restored to the shape they wanted (acquire 2 arms recorded → 7, hygiene 4 → 12) so the FIX is proved rather than the workaround, and one reach assertion makes that workaround un-reinstatable: the tree must contain at least one multi-line declaration. Battery 98/98 (5468), the +15 over REC-50's 5453 attributed by diffing per-suite lines — EXACTLY ONE SUITE MOVED, hygiene 369 → 384, the new self-test; `node scripts/coverage.mjs --strict` run directly, exit 0, **98/98 suites declaring a control and 251 arms stated**. FOURTEEN REACH ASSERTIONS, every one a DELTA or a read of the REAL corpus and never an absolute a deaf detector could also satisfy — REC-48's reach assertion was wrong for precisely that reason and this is the sixth time this project has needed the discipline. **CONDUCT'S DETERMINATION on the one call the worker left open:** the self-test STAYS in `hygiene.test.mjs` rather than becoming a 99th suite. A suite whose subject is the instrument that counts suites would move that instrument's own denominator, and hygiene.test.mjs is already the source-level-hazard suite — the self-reference is smaller where it is. **REC-50's NC arms, recorded at its assertion site because this item held the header block, ARE NOW FOLDABLE** and are the first bookkeeping owed on the next acquire.test.mjs edit.

### REC-49 · done
milestone: M10
scope: **The published INDEX tells the truth about a case's strengths — two defects, one region, both found by UI-29 and both introduced by REC-44's landing.** (a) **A REGRESSION IN SHIPPED CODE, and the more serious of the two:** `publishedManifest()`'s bundle rows lost their `manifest` column when completeness/manifest moved to `published_cases`, so the public index renders "carries no frozen strength pair" for EVERY case on EVERY real instance. A record understating what it holds is still a record that does not say what is true, and this one looks like modesty, which is why nobody would question it. Restore the column at the case altitude it now lives at. (b) `publishedManifest()` selects `strength`/`required` on neither the bundle rows nor `cases[]`, so the index has no pairs to show at all while an edition is incomplete — potentially days. **CONDUCT'S DETERMINATION, made not returned:** the index SHALL carry the per-finding pair for every RATIFIED member, awaiting window or not. It is not a composition and no new claim is made — `published_bundles.strength` is the member's own signed, ratified pair, and REC-44 already ruled that the findings which ratified are published and answerable now. What the index must NEVER do is carry a case-level pair, ratified or not (DEC-44). The awaiting window then shows the pairs it honestly has beside the roster of what it is still waiting for.

### UI-33 · done
milestone: M8
scope: **The half of the pre-authentication vocabulary that NO answer to DEC-49 will fix (UI-31's measurement, routed as a queue question rather than raised as a ruling).** UI-31 measured 13 plane-vocabulary terms standing on pre-auth surfaces, and **8 of them are the SURFACE'S OWN words** — `MEMBER_TOKEN` printed as a field label on the gate, `handle`, `CORS` in `teach()`'s unreachable-plane fallback, `R2` in the design preview's prose, `manifest`, and the surface-authored halves of `sha256`, `op=` and `this instance`. DEC-49 decides who owns the wording the PLANE sends; answer (a) rewords the plane and answer (b) licenses surfaces to translate what the plane sent — **a surface's own vocabulary is outside both**, so if the intent is "fix what a member reads before signing in", that ruling alone will not do it. This item is the other half and does NOT wait on it. Reword what this surface authors, for a reader who has not signed in and may never have seen this system: a printed `MEMBER_TOKEN` label is the clearest case and `CORS` is the second. Leave every plane-sourced term exactly as it is — touching one pre-empts DEC-49, which is the error UI-31 refused to make.

milestone: M8
scope: **The public verification surface renders a plane REFUSAL as a substantive negative (D-195, measured by UI-36).** `pubVerify` calls `op=verify` through `apiQ`, which — unlike `rec` — does not throw on `ok:false`. So a refusal envelope has no `published` field, falls to the else branch, and an uncredentialed reader is told **"NOT PUBLISHED. No published part answers to that hash."** when the plane actually declined the request. CONDUCT verified both ends: `op=verify` refuses a malformed hash with `{ok:false, error:"verify requires sha256=<64 lowercase hex>"}` at HTTP 400, `api()` never checks status, and the container row composes its hash as `bundle_sha || ""` — so an empty hash reaches it by a click. **This is the worst shape of defect this project recognises: the record asserting something it does not know, on the one surface whose purpose is "check this without us", where the whole claim is that a stranger can verify without our cooperation.** THE FIX IS A JUDGEMENT and the item does not pre-empt it: either `apiQ` throws on `ok:false` as `rec` does — which changes every public read's error path and must be swept for callers, not assumed — or `pubVerify` reads the refusal itself. **Either way it ADDS plane wording to a pre-authentication surface, which is DEC-49's subject**, so whatever it adds must be named on the landed line AND carried into DEC-49's entry, exactly as UI-36 was required to do. Correct UI-36's refusal assertion when this lands; it is written to pin the DEFECT and says so at the site.
